### 2026-09-10 | note | door Eduface CRM | id=acti_b4yNfvyeOm5StobsfBDWciUPLBBS67AWZRDcirhU3FI
Source: "Eduface Digital Concept Proposal 13-01-26" PDF, shared by Helen King. Logged here as a text note (no file attachment support via this integration) — original PDF held by Jeroen.
Proposal: Eduface.me AI Marking Assistant pilot
Proposer: Helen King (Director of Learning Innovation, Development & Skills)
Sponsor: Kyriaki Anagnostopoulou, DVC (Deputy Vice-Chancellor)
Lead department: Learning Innovation & Skills
Date: 13 January 2026
Problem: No BSU-approved AI tool for assessment/feedback exists yet; unmanaged AI use by staff risks academic standards, data protection, and policy/Student T&C violations.
History: Previously piloted KEATH.ai — did not meet their measures of success. Jisc is running a parallel pilot of three tools (KEATH, Graide, TeacherMatic) across several institutions. Jisc has a CHEST agreement with Eduface. BSU chose to pilot Eduface next after KEATH's rejection.
Pilot scope: 6 module leaders (+ additional markers) across BBS, BSUL (Hackney), Design, and Education. ~600 students. Delivery window: February–June 2026. Led by Helen King, managed day-to-day by Susanna Romans-Roca (Academic Developer). Licensing conversation with Jisc initially involves Kyriaki Anagnostopoulou (DVC) and Helen King directly.
Commercial structure: Pilot is free. If it does NOT convert to a license, a one-off £3,280 (inc. VAT) continuation fee applies (covers AI operational costs). If it DOES convert, no continuation fee — moves to paid licensing (the €80,000/year opportunity in this pipeline).
Written measures of success (their own words, useful as Metrics/Decision Criteria evidence):
- Marking Consistency: AI vs human marking agreement within acceptable threshold
- Workload Efficiencies: effectiveness gains without compromising quality, freeing academic time
- Feedback Quality and Timeliness: faster turnaround, actionable feedback maintained
- Stakeholder Acceptance: staff and student perceptions/attitudes
- Scalability and Feasibility: barriers to wider adoption across disciplines
- Policy Readiness: policy gaps or required updates identified
Governance/decision path (stated explicitly in the doc): Pilot → if successful, new concept paper to AIPB (AI Programme Board) and DOG (Digital Oversight Group) → business case to UEB (University Executive Board) for full licensing decision. Original target: submit the business case for procurement in September 2026.
Paper process gate: contract with Eduface will only be signed after (1) BSU's DPIA is signed off, and (2) formal approval from the Digital Oversight Group. DPIA had already been submitted as of this document's date.
Case study condition: Jisc negotiated the free pilot on condition BSU shares experiences and an evaluative case study with the sector (e.g. webinars). Eduface collaborates on evaluation question design, but ethical approval and evaluation data ownership stays with BSU per their data management policy — Eduface does not control that narrative.
Other consulted: colleagues on the AI Programme Board; IT Services (Ultra integration testing); Comms (internal/external communications); TED/CEP (partner institution liaison); AGQ (policy updates post-project).
Relevance: confirms Kyriaki's seniority (DVC, named Sponsor from day one) as a strong Economic Buyer signal, gives concrete written Metrics/Decision Criteria, names actual competing tools (KEATH already rejected; Graide and TeacherMatic as live alternatives in the Jisc pool), and flags that the original internal target was a September 2026 business case submission — worth checking directly whether that's still on track.

### 2026-09-10 | note | door Eduface CRM | id=acti_8xA2QG4m3284aQ3SKLSrglfE4Dp7XPyWj9DKEUiQgmj
Context: Helen flagged budget caution for the new academic/financial year ("likely to have an influence on whether or not we can take forward any new digital applications"), report going to AI Programme Board 17 Sept. Built this to prepare a value case, parked for now, pick up before/after the 17th.
OBJECTION TO KEEP IN MIND: the January "BSU Digital Concept" proposal's own primary framing is risk/governance (unmanaged/unapproved AI use risking academic standards, data protection, Student T&Cs), not cost. Workload Efficiency is one of six success criteria, not the lead motivator, and it's explicitly framed as freeing time for "more personalised student support" (reinvestment), not cost-cutting. Also: per the deal note, once DOG approves, BSU writes its own business case without Eduface (conflict-of-interest firewall) — so hand over methodology/data points Helen can use, not a finished polished case she inserts wholesale. Lead with risk/governance, use cost as supporting evidence, not the headline.
TIME IMPACT (from pilot report, graded courses MD01+MD02 only, 366 submissions — pass/fail MD03/MD04 excluded, per the report's own methodology, since those run longer for rubric-complexity reasons unrelated to the tool):
- Weighted avg Eduface review time: 2m28s
- + conservative 2-min buffer (some markers may have only edited grades without reading the submission in Eduface — flag this explicitly if used in writing)
- = 4m27s buffered Eduface time
- vs. report's own conservative baseline (low end of stated 15–30 min traditional marking): 15 min
- Time saved per submission before extra margin: ~10.5 min
- With a further ~20% safety margin on top: ~8 min saved per submission (headline, defensible number)
Scaled at 8 min/submission:
- Per 100 submissions: ~13.3 hours
- Per 366 submissions (this pilot's actual graded volume, one semester): ~49 hours — the strongest number, demonstrated not projected
- Per 1,000 submissions: ~133 hours
No BSU-wide submission/enrollment total available anywhere in Close or the documents — do not extrapolate to "university-wide" without getting that number from Helen/Susana first.
COST SCENARIO (illustrative only, hourly-paid staff, NOT all BSU marking — do not present as a BSU-wide spend figure):
Researched BSU's staffing model (2026-09-10): BSU uses both hourly-paid Associate Lecturers (formal Hourly Paid Casual Worker Agreement) AND permanent staff under a Workload Allocation Management System (WAMS). For hourly-paid staff, time saved = real reduced spend. For permanent/WAMS staff, time saved = freed capacity, not reduced spend (matches BSU's own "reinvest in more student support" framing). Found a real matched rate: Associate Lecturer in Law, £19.23/hour — ties directly to MD02 (Law) in the pilot.
- Per 100 submissions: ~£256
- Per 366 submissions (this pilot, one semester): ~£942
- Per 1,000 submissions: ~£2,563
Keep this scenario visibly separate from the time/capacity numbers — don't blend into one BSU-wide £ figure, the real hourly/permanent staff split is unknown.
QUALITY / RISK IMPACT (non-time, supports the governance-first framing):
- ~94% average AI grading accuracy across graded courses
- 2,300+ structured, rubric-aligned feedback items delivered across 435 submissions
- Every student received feedback against every rubric criterion, every time
- Tutors changed the AI-suggested grade in 70–97% of cases in most groups — strong evidence of retained academic judgement, not automation-only
STILL MISSING to strengthen this further: BSU's own pre-pilot baseline marking time (not just the report's generic 15–30 min assumption) and an hourly rate for permanent/WAMS staff — ask Helen or Susana, ideally via their Workload Allocation Management System (WAMS) data if they have it documented, same approach that worked well with Irma at Breederode.

## Meetings (summary + user_note)

### 2026-07-16 | meeting | Eduface: Blackboard demo prep discussion | id=acti_3cVRJ42Au1OHjIPieRxdL9c7hT26rV9i5K5TmE2oPZ6
Deelnemers: Helen King, jeroen.van.gessel@eduface.me, menno.hahury, Steven Lewarne, Susana Romans-Roca
SUMMARY:
- Prepared Blackboard integration demo focusing on staff efficiencies, two-way syncing with Turnitin, and multi-stakeholder workflow views (markers, moderators, module leaders, external examiners).
- Confirmed integration works with Blackboard Ultra, Canvas, and Moodle with automatic assignment routing to both Turnitin and Eduface.
- Emphasized critical demo requirements: human-first assessment approach, usage monitoring dashboards, moderation capabilities, and eight-year data retention for compliance.
- Identified key adoption barriers as time pressures and learning curves, requiring demo to emphasize ease of use, reliability, and integration seamlessness.
- Demo will be conducted in Bath Spa's environment with 10-12 participants including pilot module leaders, digital learning developers, academic IT staff, and recorded for broader sharing.
### Meeting Purpose and Attendee Introductions
- This meeting served as preparation for the formal Blackboard integration demo with Bath Spa University.
- A note taker was included to ensure proper documentation of discussion points.
- Jeroen van Gessel introduced himself as Eduface co-founder focusing on business operations, started almost three and a half years ago.
- Menno presented as co-founder with technical background in software engineering and AI who built the platform with Samuel.
- Samuel introduced himself as lead software engineer at Eduface, joining in February.
- Helen King identified herself as Director of Learning, Innovation, Development and Skills, serving as strategic lead for the pilot project.
- Steven Lewarne described himself as Senior Digital Learning Developer supporting academic staff.
- Susana Romans-Roca presented as Academic Developer managing the day-to-day pilot operations.
### Demo Content and Integration Requirements
- The demo must provide evidence supporting Helen's paper to the AIPB meeting about integration benefits and efficiencies for staff.
- Jeroen confirmed the integration works with Canvas and Moodle in addition to Blackboard Ultra and will be shown during the demo.
- The demo should showcase automations syncing back to Blackboard and bidirectional syncs from Blackboard to Eduface to reduce setup friction.
- Jeroen explained that when students submit assignments in Blackboard, they automatically sync to both Turnitin for originality checks and Eduface for AI feedback.
- Susana noted that Turnitin has introduced a new Standard Assignment Tool with Blackboard integration, potentially eliminating friction by avoiding manual processes.
- The demo should demonstrate how assignments automatically pass to Turnitin's new Standard Assignment Tool, though activation status needs verification in the test server.
### Role-Based Workflow and Marking Considerations
- **The demo must show the tool from multiple stakeholder perspectives: markers, moderators, module leaders, and external examiners.**
- **Friction and ease of use are critical factors—any added complexity discourages staff adoption.**
- Steven emphasized that uptime and availability build confidence in integrations, noting the successful adoption of Turnitin LTI despite its different interface.
- Susana highlighted that staff currently perceive Turnitin as integrated with Blackboard Ultra due to its seamless functionality, even though it is a separate integration.
- The demo should demonstrate compatibility with Blackboard Ultra's grade journeys to ensure marks sync properly without manual entry.
- The workflow must support moderation processes where moderators can view staff submissions and module leaders can maintain oversight across multiple markers.
### Data Retention and Compliance Requirements
- **Retention policies are essential—the tool must retain all data for institutional appeals and external compliance with UK Office for Students requirements of five years post-graduation.**
- Bath Spa retains materials for three additional years beyond the five-year requirement, totaling eight years of necessary data retention.
- The demo should address retention mechanisms to ensure this compliance box is ticked for university adoption.
### Governance and Approval Process
- Helen's paper goes through the AIPB for input and recommendations, then to the Digital Oversight Group in August for oversight of new software proposals.
- The Digital Oversight Group uses a prioritization matrix to determine next steps and may request additional information before approval.
- Following the Digital Oversight Group decision, the project may progress to the University Executive Board for funding approval if needed.
- The Executive Board meets monthly, so timing depends on when the proposal reaches that stage.
### Demo Attendees and Technical Setup
- Attendees should include module leaders from the pilot, digital learning developers, Susana, and academic IT staff for approximately 10-12 participants.
- The demo should be conducted in Bath Spa's environment rather than Eduface's sandbox for greater authenticity.
- Steve from Bath Spa IT will be available to navigate the Blackboard environment during the demo, with Susana and Steven as backup.
- The demo should be recorded for reference and sharing with pilot participants who cannot attend, including possibly Kiriaki who was active in early meetings.
- Scheduling will be handled via email coordination due to summer leave periods and multiple calendars to check.
### Monitoring and Pedagogical Safeguards
- **The demo should include dashboards and metrics showing usage data to verify that staff actively engage with the tool rather than over-relying on AI feedback.**
- Moderators should be able to see evidence of human interaction and decision-making in the marking process.
- **The tool must support a human-first assessment approach, ensuring educators retain active involvement in evaluation decisions.**
- Usage monitoring allows university staff and moderators to track time spent and verify appropriate human oversight.
### Formative and Summative Assessment Use Cases
- The pilot includes two distinct use cases: modules using summative assessment and the ASK team using the tool formatively.
- For formative student feedback, Eduface should provide a button in Blackboard allowing students to request feedback without summative grading.
- If student-facing formative feedback integrates through Blackboard Ultra, it should be included in the demo for consistency.
- Helen requested focusing the demo on the academic/module use case with summative assessment, then briefly mentioning the formative student-facing approach to save time.
### Staff Adoption Concerns and System Pressures
- Staff at Bath Spa are time-poor and under pressure, making steep learning curves a barrier to adoption.
- The university continuously adds new systems, creating additional burden on staff unless clear time benefits are demonstrated.
- Systems showing tangible time savings and reduced workload are more readily received by academic staff.
- Staff acceptance improves when implementations feel integrated and supported rather than adding extra steps to existing workflows.

### 2026-05-14 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_emPgEmHbfDTbFwN0rF5rEhH5qqS581On0B8vFEk8Rxs
Onderwerp: PGCE Primary
Hi Jeroen
Unfortunately, one of the PGCE Primary tutors, JH, has decided not to continue her participation in the pilot as she mentions the following issues:
  *
Quick mark comments are not logically listed and are in a small window so take too long to find what you want.
  *
It often isn't possible to select text from beginning and end of assignment so I am doing a less than complete marking job
  *
The style of feedback and grading of 7B and 7D is often failed and creates a dissonance with our current style and policy (eg 2nd person) which then feels either inaccurate or takes too much time to edit
I've written back to Niamh to offer support to see if it's possible to get this tutor back but her decision may be final. Niamh says she hasn't heard from the other tutors, she assumes all is going well for them.
I can see that one has already marked all the submissions in her box, for example. The fact that JH is withdrawing is disappointing but, hopefully, an exception.
It would be good for us to understand her feedback. I'll try to get in touch to find out what she means by "quickmarks not logically listed" and how this and the other points she mentions could be improved.
For now, c
[...afgekapt...]

Hi Jeroen,

I am struggling to ensure generated feedback includes more of a focus on 'positives' or even neutral comments.  I have tested adapting the tone and varied the instructions.  Feedforward areas for development appear generally appropriate at the moment.  I currently include this instruction for each of the rubric criterion at present:
     *
"Write feedback in a formal, impersonal academic tone, referring only to "the assignment" or "this assignment." Do not use "you," "your," or address the student directly at any point.
For each rubric criterion, generate exactly three sentences in this order:
1) A positive comment
2) A second, different positive comment
3) One feed forward comment beginning exactly with "Feed forward:"
Each criterion must be assessed holistically. The listed points are indicative and should not be treated as a checklist. The feedback should provide a balanced, integrated judgement of overall performance, recognising strengths alongside areas for development. [...afgekapt...]

### 2026-04-30 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_hBLigqD4l0MevcuP2cEUunpb3dqyROag9YCemsONVCE
Onderwerp: PGP7011 Primary - Pilot participation
Hi Jeroen
Niamh McGrogan, the module leader,  has confirmed this module's participation in the pilot. Due to availability, only some tutors will be using Eduface, not all of them. We've gone through the rational for this and it has been approved by Helen.
There are 90 students in the module, they estimate that around 30 of them will be uploaded to Eduface.
Task: Assessment 4 Essay and portfolio
Submission deadline: 11th May noon
Niamh has requested if we could set up the onboarding meeting tomorrow at 14:00. Would this be possible?
The assessment task
This is a post-grad module with a slightly different set up to the others.
Students teach in schools and submit an essay and a portfolio of work (all in one document).
The portfolio includes images of handwritten content. Would this be a limitation?
The assessment is PASS/FAIL, students do not receive a percentage for their marks.
Marking criteria and supporting doc
I'm attaching the marking criteria that they use in this email, together with the tutors' guide to marking this task.
They also have some quickmarks.
Calibrating the model
Although Niamh is the module leader, she doesn't do  any marking. Her role is that of overall co-ordi
[...afgekapt...]

Hi Jeroen,
Thank you for sharing this with us. The update looks very interesting, and I appreciate you organising the marking in a way that means I won’t need to review the document twice.
Please see below a snapshot of the guidance I will be providing to moderators when marking student work. Would it be possible to explore whether the system could be trained using this?
Best wishes,
Mani
What Answers Look Like Across Grade Bands
70+ First Class Answer
A 70+ answer gives a confident, critical and well-structured response to the question. It does not simply describe the law on parenthood. Instead, it develops a clear argument about the extent to which the legal framework in England and Wales recognises legal, biological and social parenthood.
A strong First Class answer will usually begin by explaining that parenthood is not only about biology. It will recognise that legal parenthood, biological connection and social or psychological parenting may overlap, but they do not always do so. The answer may use Re G to explain these different dimensions of parenthood and then use that distinction throughout the essay.
A 70+ answer would show that the law is effective in some areas because 
[...afgekapt...]

The tutors have suggested the following dates and times for onboarding meetings:
Friday 1st May at 9:30 Meeting with Ash (who will coordinate this and will be doing the initial calibration, Sarah is the module leader but will not be directly participating in the marking)
Tuesday 5th May at 9:00 For a follow up meeting and demo to the other two tutors.
I'm waiting for confirmation of Helen's approval. If you are ok with this set up and timings, would you be available to meet at the suggested times or do we need to find times?
I believe Sarah sent you the rubric and you already have the quickmarks they use. Is there anything else that you'd need?
Many thanks
Susanna

### 2026-04-08 | email in | van Helen King <h.king@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_LsY3EUkDnOeeXRc858antNabcVWn6CyVbfxNhrxipw1
Onderwerp: Re: Webinar on 16th April
Hi Jeroen,
Good to know there's interest in the webinar. I'll be working on my presentation on Friday and will share with you then. My intention is to focus on good pedagogy / good practice in assessment and feedback, and how we think an AI-assisted tool might help with that. At this stage, I won't be sharing the details of the pilot (e.g. module names or subject areas), but I will share the positive outcomes that have already arisen (e.g. conversations about rubrics, surfacing less-good practice). I will also share the challenges, which are the same as for the other Jisc pilots, in terms of perceptions and conceptions of the use of AI tools in assessment. So, my presentation will be tool 'agnostic'. At a follow-up webinar, after we have completed the pilot and the evaluative case study, I can then share more specific info about our experience with Eduface.
Best wishes,
Helen
Professor Helen King PhD NTF SFSEDA PFHEA
(Pronouns she/her)
Director of Learning Innovation, Development & Skills, Bath Spa University

### 2026-04-02 | email in | van Giles Martin <g.martin@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_5BtUbLSFbs5WGbANqnL4oC4QSbID8OHo4F9ZaGEDljZ
Onderwerp: Re: Preparation for Eduface meeting this afternoon
Hi Jeroen,
In case John isn't only in time to reply otherwise, some quick details:
We're planning on using eduface for the compulsory but formative lit review they submit ahead of full planning and implementation of an innovation project in their teaching in the autumn. Students here = our own staff (primarily lecturers). Hence opportunity for them to see things from both angles too. They submit the lit review, have a week asynchonours in groups to comment and feedback (peer), then John does (tutor), with this adding a third dimension. As the module is on tech and innovation in teaching, we'll be discussing with them even more. They do then submit the lit review again summatively a few weeks later.
From Handbook, details below (John may be able to add more of guidance he gives, but I'd guess suitable for you purposes for now). FYI they've already identified a suitable pedagogic challenge or similar in their teaching they wish to address.
Best wishes,
Giles
     *
Critical review of a specific innovation in theory and practice (1800 words)
This assignment takes the form of a critical review of your specific innovation in theory and practice. You will have to find academic literature
[...afgekapt...]

### 2026-03-25 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_U2XO6TXVbkHLvt0gfMt4jzpmvudh1x9NSMkl4WNWGHm
Onderwerp: Use of AI Environmental Impact
Hi Jeroen
We’ve recently received student concerns about the environmental impact of using Eduface for assessment. This point has come up before in discussions about the pilot and it’s clearly an issue that resonates with both students and staff.
The UCU (academic staff union) in their newly published UCU Principles for Use of Artificial Intelligence<https://www.ucu.org.uk/media/15656/Principles-for-use-of-artificial-intelligence/pdf/UCU_AI_principles_March_26.pdf> suggests conducting an environmental impact assessment on any new AI tools.
With this in mind, it'd be worth considering how to address this concern within the pilot and in any future implementation.
Is environmental impact something you are considering or already have in place?
Many thanks
Susanna

"Join Professor Helen King and Jeroen van Gessel for an overview of Bath Spa University’s pilot of Eduface, an AI-assisted feedback tool designed and trained specifically for higher education. The session will outline the drivers behind the pilot, including exploring the potential of Eduface as an additional approved tool in the University's toolbox to deliver an excellent student experience (i.e. through supporting consistent, high-quality, and timely feedback aligned with policy, data protection, and academic standards). Helen will reflect on the goals, and early considerations of introducing AI assistants into assessment and feedback practices. Eduface will demo their AI-assisted feedback solution."

### 2026-03-12 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_EVMg9ypiFXLYCOGy93j1OhlGrenLGorJwUCwP7yXO3X
Onderwerp: Re: Marking upload update
Hi Jaya and Jeroen
These are my thoughts on this.
Given that the AI feedback assistant is not actually the one marking the paper but only providing suggestions, I don't think that the AI suggesting a mark of 69% should be a major issue. I would not change the way it works.
As a marker, it can be useful to see a 69, as it can flag that the paper/band  is borderline, therefore requiring closer consideration by the tutor. The human marker should be the one taking this type of decisions rather than the AI.
Regards,
Susanna

### 2026-03-11 | email in | van Jayavarthini Govindaraj <j.govindaraj@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_Qbqs8dtZz9cdsqD7RHIiz1wKgY0A42T1y9ZG1bDEaaI
Onderwerp: Re: Marking upload update
Hi Jeroen,
I would like to update you that I have now marked two scripts from the current submissions. Please find the key points below:
  *
The marks generated are broadly in line with what I would have awarded. However, one script received a score of 69. As a reminder, we do not provide marks ending in 9 (e.g., 59 or 69). These should be rounded to the nearest even number, either 68 or 70. Could we please adjust this within the system?
  *
I noticed that a few words are still appearing in American English. For example, organized. I amended these manually.
  *
I made minor edits to the language and phrasing of the feedback in a few places for clarity and tone. For example, rewording suggestions such as “you could have added…, and that could help you achieve higher marks.”
At this stage, the folder can now be duplicated so that I can assign the scripts to the markers. I am meeting them tomorrow at 13:00, please update me so that it will be very helpful.
Please let me know if you need any additional information from my side. Thank you.
Best Regards,
Jaya Govindaraj
Senior Lecturer/Programme Leader - June intake - Business and Management
Bath Spa University London

[...afgekapt...]

Onderwerp: Contract ready to sign
Hi Jeroen,
We have now finalised and signed the contract for the Eduface pilot. We have made a few minor amendments:
  *
emphasised that the evaluative case study will be led by BSU;
  *
highlighted that we will gather student feedback as well as staff;
  *
I've copied the 'Purpose' bullets in Section 1 to the Purpose section of Appendix 3;
  *
Added a clause 7.5 regarding the intellectual property of any data (e.g. assessment rubrics and student assignments) we submit to Eduface.
The ​contract is available here<https://bathspaonline-my.sharepoint.com/:w:/r/personal/h_king_bathspa_ac_uk/Documents/Documents/2025-26%20Projects/Generative%20AI/Eduface/Final%20pilot%20agreement%20Eduface%20x%20BSU.docx?d=w07900cd5afb94baba792f97fe3983382&csf=1&web=1&e=fCwC0F>​, ready for your signature on pages 8 and 12.
Best wishes,

### 2026-02-24 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_ovGjimfxgXXpJwBiRQja6O40XL7fi2Z0ONXo5wUfe98
Onderwerp: Eduface Module Leader training
Dear colleagues
We are pleased to let you know that the Data Protection Impact Assessment (DPIA) for the pilot has now been formally signed off and the contract will be in place shortly meaning that we are set to begin very soon .
As part of the pilot setup, Jeroen van Gessel from Eduface (copied here) will be contacting you to arrange two 45 minute online meetings:
  *
The first one, will be an introductory meeting in which you’ll be signed up to Eduface and where Jerone will provide a walkthrough of the platform so you get familiar with its features and marking workflow.
  *
A second meeting will take place ahead of your assignment deadline to ensure everything is configured correctly and ready for use.
For modules with multiple markers, the training for the rest of the markers will take place separately. Eduface will arrange a webinar after the sessions with the Module Leader and will take the form of a webinar supplemented by video instructions.
Do not hesitate to get in touch if you have any questions in the meantime.
Best wishes,
Susanna

Hi Jeroen,
The draft contract has been checked by our legal team. There are two recommended amendments (clauses 3.1d and 8.2), and Appendix 3 (measures of success) needs to be attached. NB for legal's information (cc-ed): the Terms & Conditions were shared with IT Services and considered as part of the DPIA. I don't know if the data processing agreement was also shared by Eduface, @Jeroen<mailto:jeroen.van.gessel@eduface.me> can you confirm?

### 2026-02-06 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_2rKEgrWgtrvwdwHFfeMLnLcERBgzaRBidGXVGTTCaRF
Onderwerp: A couple of queries
Hi Jeroen and Menno
I hope you're well.
I have a couple of queries that have come from tutors:
1. Use of QuickMark comment banks
Tutors have asked whether it is possible to use their existing Turnitin QuickMark comment sets within Eduface. If you remember, this was raised when you came to BSU for the introductory meeting. One of the PGCE teams currently uses a shared QuickMark set in Turnitin to support consistency across multiple markers. They would like to know whether this set can be imported into Eduface.
The QuickMark set is a .qms file.
2. Sandbox environment for tutors and myself
There is also a question about whether it would be possible to set up a sandbox for tutors to experience Eduface ahead of live marking. Module leaders for large cohorts with multiple markers are concerned that marking may initially take longer, some suggesting piloting Eduface with a sample of submissions only.
We feel that full participation would be preferable and wondered whether giving tutors hands on experience in advance might help address these concerns. I would be happy to create dummy student submissions for this purpose if a sandbox can be set up.
3. Guidance and workflow documentation
It 
[...afgekapt...]

### 2026-02-04 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_gYoDSchbST7X2asQ42aozsIqgRizSnj7WJJBz99xswE
Onderwerp: Re: Pilot Contract
Hi
I think Turnitin's response is disappointing.
Turnitin already has an integration along these lines within Blackboard Ultra Assignments, where Turnitin reports can be generated directly from papers submitted to a Blackboard assignment itself without the need to upload assignments in Ultra to two different platforms (Turnitin and Ultra). I imagine you'd like to set up something similar were the originality check (Turnitin) is embedded in Eduface.
It would be worth looking at how others in your situation may be navigating this issue. I suspect you must not be the only ones Turnitin sees as competitors.
I'm thinking of Graide.
As far as I know, there is no supported integration between Graide and Turnitin. Graide has gone for a different similarity/originality provider called Inspera Originality that will be embedded directly within the Graide platform, without the need to export files to third-party similarity tools. This was announced last December. See Inspera announcement<https://inspera.com/press-releases/graide-and-inspera-announce-strategic-partnership/> "This collaboration will embed Inspera Originality directly into the Graide platform, offering customers a seamless and se
[...afgekapt...]

### 2026-02-04 | email in | van Helen King <h.king@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_xLt7aEiGgOZAVNVRkL6jQkxcKuJMVYCsQH80wLHoUVj
Onderwerp: Re: Pilot Contract
Hi Jeroen,
I wonder if Turnitin misunderstood your positioning of Eduface. The words you use in your email are 'integrating Turnitin within Eduface'. What you mean is having an Eduface integration with Turnitin, so that documents that have been processed through Turnitin for similarity checking can then be seamlessly transferred into Eduface for marking.
I've copied in Susanna, to see if she has any suggestions about wording. It might also be worth you discussing this with Jisc to see if they can help mediate the conversation with Turnitin or, at least, provide an endorsement of what you want to do.
Best wishes,
Helen
Professor Helen King PhD NTF SFSEDA PFHEA
(Pronouns she/her)
Director of Learning Innovation, Development & Skills, Bath Spa University

### 2026-02-04 | email in | van Helen King <h.king@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_3Ri7BmFGdsnH0QipBdipHrLe9ZbsbvibdeljqOKstD1
Onderwerp: Pilot Contract
Hi Jeroen,
I contacted Hannah about the contract and she confirmed that this should come directly from you. Can you draft something now, so we're ready to go when we get final sign-off, and can you ensure it includes or appends the additional relevant documents as mentioned by our Data Protection Officer:
"The DPO sought and obtained assurances that Eduface will not use BSU student data for model training, cannot access other students’ submissions, and does not hold a comparison repository. Public Task was recommended as the lawful basis. Additional risks around re‑identification, AI accuracy and student understanding have been mitigated through prompts, human oversight and clear communication. The DPO also required SCCs, the UK Addendum/IDTA, a TRA and contractual guarantees on transient processing. Subject to final security review and contractual safeguards, the DPO advises the pilot can proceed."
Many thanks!
Helen

### 2026-01-27 | email in | van Susana Romans-Roca <s.romans-roca@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_iHFgLWHw37U9mszCFTe4AkN2NAWML3e8UnlseI1KJEv
Onderwerp: Re: Queries for DPIA
Thank you, Jeroen.
Helen, will Jeroen's solution work for the pilot?
Our current assessment platforms (Turnitin/Blackboard) hide all identifiers, student name and surname and also BSU student ID so that it is not possible for markers to identify who the student is when the assignment is set to be marked anonymously.
For example, Turnitin gives each submission a Turnitin ID, so that this is separate from the student's BSU ID when assignments are set to be marked anonymously. Under these conditions, names and student BSU IDs are only revealed after the marks are posted.
Jeroen/Menno, would something like this be possible with Eduface for the pilot without the integration with Ultra?
Many thanks
Susanna

### 2026-01-26 | email in | van Helen King <h.king@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_7HyxBuMXxxfS5qfcxG25iWsR7mb7d07OysVyvomqwb3
Onderwerp: Queries for DPIA
Hi Jeroen, Menno & Susanna,
Pippa in our legal team has the following queries for the Eduface DPIA, please can you provide responses:
  1.  Eduface: From the public information, it looks as though Eduface operates its own AI language model trained on student-generated writing datasets, rather than outsourcing AI model development to third-parties. Please can you confirm that BSU student data submitted during the pilot is not used for training, fine-tuning or model improvements.
  2.  Eduface: Please confirm that you cannot access other students’ submissions and does not hold a repository enabling cross-student comparisons.
  3.  Eduface & Susanna: can there be automated or other prompts to staff and students to remind students not to embed personal identifiers in submissions? And is it possible to mitigate against the inclusion of personal or sensitive information e.g. marker checks, automated detection?
  4.
Eduface: please confirm that this suggested risk and mitigation is appropriate:
Risk: Data sent for text extraction is processed transiently by a U.S. sub‑processor, creating a restricted international transfer with potential exposure to non‑adequate jurisdiction risks.
Mitiga
[...afgekapt...]

### 2025-12-17 | email in | van Helen King <h.king@bathspa.ac.uk> | id=acti_qqqZH7FNYWrhq03FeB2BW1bFE7iCTCePeC8F4GQxOlV
Onderwerp: RE: BSU DPIA for Eduface
Hi Menno,
These are the measures of success that we had for our previous AI marking assistant pilot:
  *   Marking Consistency: Agreement between AI and human marking within an acceptable threshold.
  *   Workload Reduction: Reduction in marking time without compromising quality.
  *   Feedback Quality and Timeliness: Faster turnaround while maintaining actionable feedback.
  *   Stakeholder Acceptance: Perceptions and attitudes from staff and students.
  *   Scalability and Feasibility: Understanding barriers to wider adoption across disciplines.
  *   Policy Readiness: Identification of policy gaps or required updates.
The first three points are most relevant to the direct users (academic staff and students), so would be important to include in the January workshop. The other points are important from a strategic, wider implementation perspective. Are these a good fit for the Eduface pilot from your perspective?
Best wishes,
Helen
Professor Helen King PhD NTF SFSEDA PFHEA
(Pronouns she/her)
Director of Learning Innovation, Development & Skills, Bath Spa University
